# Layout review of the heater wall board

The review of `hardware/sunbeam-em7000/heater-wall-board` by the [Layout review](../../../docs/board-design-method.md#layout-review) step of the board design method. Each round is recorded at its end, before any change to the board it calls for. Round 1 was judged by one reviewer, a session that did not design the board, from the board's design files, the checklist (the catalogue's 70 items and the board's own items BOARD-1 to BOARD-18 of `layout-plan.md`), the layout review views, the placement view, the pre-order check's lines and the committed `layout-plan.md`.

## Round 1

- **Board judged**: 6e36597d4db364a581aadd6964c736611ec3e946, the commit holding the design files as they stand: the router's commit b5f2547d with text-only property changes (the opto-triac part fields).
- **Views**: the commands the views were rendered with, as run from a detached worktree of the commit outside the repository (`git worktree add --detach <scratch>/wall-r1 6e36597d4db364a581aadd6964c736611ec3e946`; `<scratch>` is the reviewer's scratch folder), with `KICAD_CLI=/Applications/KiCad/KiCad.app/Contents/MacOS/kicad-cli`; the classes are the critical classes routing locked:

```
(cd <scratch>/wall-r1/hardware/sunbeam-em7000/heater-wall-board && task hw:views -- --board heater-wall-board.kicad_pcb --project heater-wall-board.kicad_pro --class Mains --class MainsTrunk --class MainsBranch --out <scratch>/hwb-views-1)
(cd <scratch>/wall-r1/hardware/sunbeam-em7000/heater-wall-board && task hw:placement -- --board heater-wall-board.kicad_pcb --project heater-wall-board.kicad_pro --out <scratch>/hwb-placement-1)
```

  The views command wrote 75 images, their index and the fact table (30 px/mm, tiles of 4 rows by 1 column for the whole-board views); the placement command wrote 4 images and `measures.txt` (LENGTH 373.5906 mm, CROSSINGS 7, OUTSIDE 0). Two reviewer-made views, rendered with Pillow from the same views folder, were used only to look at tiles together: contact sheets of the tiles named in the rows; every verdict cites the tile files themselves. No further view was rendered.
- **Pre-order check**: the board's `DESIGN` line and every `FIGURE` and `VIOLATION` line under it, from `python3 hardware/tools/board_check.py` run on the detached worktree of the commit with the check's own `check_design` held to the wall design only (the check's own entry point with the other designs skipped, so that the wall board's lines are read in minutes; the same code that `task hw:verify` runs on it). `task hw:verify` itself was started on the same worktree and had not finished when this round was written (its main-board part runs long); the closing round runs it whole. The lines:


### Plan and free pass

**Plan against layout.** The floorplan holds: the four mains rectangles and the two low-voltage rectangles are drawn as rule areas on both copper layers at the plan's bounds, every footprint lies in the rectangles that list it (`measures.txt` OUTSIDE 0), and the connector table holds (pin 1 of J911, J921, J931, J941 at the table positions, J931 on the right edge). The outline is the plan's rectangle (20.0, 21.0) to (65.0, 96.0) with the two 1.5 mm slots open at the right edge; the top edge stands back 1.0 mm from the plate plane, as the placement section records. The yes rows and the board's own items were read against the views, each by its verdict row; the departures are findings 1.1 to 1.9 (finding numbers below). The plan rows that still say DIP-6, that describe an OUT_C spur and a via on the OUT_S spur, or that give R921 at y 73.70 (it stands at y 72.50 since placement candidate 3) are frozen history and are not raised. BOARD-18 exists as a row and is judged (88 items in all: 70 catalogue items and BOARD-1 to BOARD-18). BOARD-15's words "the right edge holds nothing but the two slots between y 42 and y 80" read literally would fail on U911, J931 and U921, which stand at the right edge by the floorplan and the connector table; the row's stated purpose is that no case material bridges a slot mouth, a case-step input, and it is judged on that purpose, with no finding.

**The 5 V net.** `+5V_WB` (class Supply5V, routed in the unlocked router commit with script-written copper) matches the plan's intent and its rules: J931 pin 1 to U921 pin 1 straight down x 63.18 on F.Cu; to U911 pin 1 on B.Cu (63.18, 58.2) to (61.78, 56.8) to (58.1, 56.8), one via at (58.1, 56.8) of 0.7 mm over a 0.3 mm drill, then F.Cu to the pad; 0.2 mm tracks, none under 1.67 mm long; the via stands 0.2 mm from the J931 pin 3 pad edge (the class clearance) and its B.Cu ring is far from Mains copper; the report shows no `track_width`, `via_dangling` or corner line. No finding.

**Free pass.** All 75 views and the 4 placement tiles were looked at (the tiles of the layer, net and class views one by one for the copper layers, the silk, the edge, the assembly and the placement views; the mask, paste, courtyard, fabrication, comment and back-side layers, and the remaining net tiles, on contact sheets of their tiles). Seen that no item or plan row asks about: the board carries no silkscreen text but the 15 references: no legend names which of J941, J921 and J911 is RET, OUT_S or OUT_C, none names the three pins of J931 (+5V, coffee LED return, steam LED return), and no board name or revision is drawn, where the main board carries 162 legend texts including a mains warning (finding 1.10). The three blades are identical parts on a mains board that the owner fits by hand. Nothing else: no hook, loop, stub or via thicket (one via), the necks are the only wandering of copper (finding 1.1), the through-hole drill marks drawn on every layer of the views are the views' own marking (they also appear on B.SilkS, F.Paste and Cmts.User) and not copper or paste. The sections of the method this round could not follow in full: the JLCPCB capabilities page (fetched 2026-11-19) carried no annular-ring row in its server-rendered text, so MFG-1's annular figure rests on the board's own row quoting it.

**Items the reviewer judged in part, with the basis stated in the row:** BOARD-7 and BOARD-8 (the parts declare no three-dimensional model, so heights and the plate's lower edge are from the records, the case step's input), POWER-3's count of layer changes (from the board file's plated holes and the zone outlines), and the neck widths of finding 1.1 (measured from the filled zone polygons in the board file, with the check's pad geometry). None is left `cannot judge`.

**Proposed closures (step 7; nothing is applied by this round).** 1.1 by `overridden by: BOARD-13`, the accepted 1.04 mm triac pin gaps, which with the 1.5 mm class clearance cap the zone at 0.58 mm round the pad, with the heating arithmetic of the neck (about 1 mm of 0.8 to 1.0 mm by 35 um copper at 5.38 A beside the 1.5 mm pad barrel) and the fuse let-through open item; the strip widths and the OUT_C stretch by a change to the board or by `met:` with the arithmetic. 1.2 and 1.3 by `overridden by: BOARD-16` (no Mains via, the pin-swap decision), 1.3 with the note that the pins carry gate current. 1.4 by `overridden by: BOARD-11` (every further hole needs 13.45 mm of mains-free board) and the owner's decision D1. 1.5 by a change to the board for `min_hole_clearance` (0.35 mm, then the check rerun on the whole board) and `overridden by: BOARD-1` for the annular width (the oval pads' 0.20 mm narrow side), or the owner's recorded acceptance of the minimum. 1.6 by a change adding the row to `design-rules.md`. 1.7 by `overridden by: BOARD-8` (the opto-triac stands at least 20 mm from the triac body). 1.8 by `overridden by: BOARD-16`, with the 1.6 mm of board between the two nets against the basis's 1.5 mm mains-to-mains clearance. 1.9 by a change drawing the keep-outs on a drawing layer, or `met:` if the owner accepts the list in `fabrication-notes.md` and BOARD-14's words are read as that. 1.10 by a change adding the legends. Each closed by a change needs the next round.

**Disclosure.** `hw:review-history` fails on this record, and no later edit can clear it: round 1's first recording (commit `3a0189465e35`) had a blank line after the separator row of each of its two tables, which cuts the tables for the history command, and the command reads every version of the record, so it reports that version and the two that followed (`5678695cfb7d`, `a932481456cb`) and every verdict row and finding as added against the unreadable recording. The text of round 1 is unchanged and was repaired in a later version; clearing the fault would need a rewrite of the commits the record cites by hash. The owner accepted the failure on 2026-11-19 ("Yes to both", to disclosing it and tracking the repair). The repair of the command and the method is tracked by `SOL-REVIEW-RECORD-HISTORY-READS-PAST-A-MALFORMED-FIRST-RECORDING`. A second failure is disclosed the same way: `round 3 order` fails because round 3's records commit (`ef6408477474`) follows the silk commit (`60c665fc312c`) that closed its findings, so its end-of-round commit does not come before every design change after the round's Board judged commit; it cannot be fixed without rewriting history, and is accepted and disclosed on the same terms. The rule for later rounds: a round's records are committed before the board commit that closes its findings. Recorded 2026-11-20 as `owner-sign-off.md` W-13: his "Yes to both" of 2026-11-19 covers the first-recording failure; the `round 3 order` failure is covered only as the same class, and his "Yes" of 2026-11-20 accepts closing the review at Round 5. A fourth item arose in the records commit `e422d9f38adb`, which recorded Round 5 with finding 5.1's row one cell short (Closed by in the Planning shortfall column), so that version and the one after it (`a7a3e5ef2d7e`) fail `round structure`; the row was corrected afterwards, which makes `round 5 as first recorded` fail too, as the first recording cannot be read. Neither can be cleared without rewriting history.

### Findings

| Finding | Raised by | Severity | What and where | Rule | Added as | Planning shortfall | Closed by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1.1 | POWER-2, BOARD-4 | function | The heavy copper narrows at the triac pins: OUT_S below T921 pin 2 and OUT_C below T911 pin 2 are 0.58 mm wide under the pad, 0.90 mm at y 24.6 and 1.5 mm only at y 25.2 (x 31.9 to 33.4 and x 56.5 to 58.0, y 24.0 to 26.0), where POWER-2 and BOARD-4 say a neck of the pad width, 1.5 mm; also the RET strip is 5.8 mm (x 21.5 to 27.3) where BOARD-4 says 6.0 mm and the OUT_C front-layer strip is absent y 69.6 to 80.9 where the row lists y 70.85 to 81.90. Views `layer-F.Cu-r1c1`, `layer-F.Cu-r2c1`, `layer-F.Cu-r3c1`, `net-_Steam_heater_switch_OUT_S-r1c1`, `net-_Coffee_heater_switch_OUT_C-r1c1`, `classes-r1c1`. | no: no rule of the five forms states it: the check measures tracks (WORK-8) and not the width of a pour at a pad, and a measured rule would need a neck-width figure with a source outside the check, which no published document gives for a heavy pour at a pad | POWER-2 and BOARD-4 (they state it) | Step 2 of Planning the layout: the row's 1.5 mm neck cannot be drawn, since the 1.5 mm class clearance to the neighbouring pad copper leaves 0.58 mm at the pad's centre line, so the row followed as written would not have met the item (amendment commit: 2560a35e); the 5.8 mm and the stretch positions: `—`, the rows could be followed |`overridden by: BOARD-1 and BOARD-13 with MAINS-1` for the necks and the RET width. The triac pads are the ovals of BOARD-1, 1.5 mm across the pad row, which is as wide as the accepted 1.04 mm gap between adjacent pins (BOARD-13) allows at the 2.54 mm pitch. Below the pad the zone keeps the 1.5 mm mains-to-mains clearance of the separation basis (MAINS-1) from the pads of pins 1 and 3 on either side, which leaves 5.08 − 1.5 − 2 × 1.5 = 0.58 mm between them at the pad, and the strip reaches 1.5 mm at y 25.2, 0.7 mm below the pad's end at y 24.51; the copper narrower than the pad is under 1 mm long. At 5.38 A a neck 1 mm long, 0.8 mm wide and 35 um thick has 1.72e-8 × 1e-3 / (0.8e-3 × 35e-6) = 0.61 milliohm and dissipates 5.38² × 0.61e-3 = 18 mW, into the pad barrel on one side and a 5 mm strip on the other. The neck's behaviour under a heater short circuit is carried by the fuse let-through open item (`open-items.md`) and the owner's list. The RET strip is 5.8 mm (x 21.5 to 27.3), not 6.0 mm, so that its edge stands 2.7 mm from the OUT_S strip at x 30.0, over the basis's 2.6 mm mains-to-mains creepage (MAINS-1); on both layers it carries 10.18 A at 5.09 A a layer, where one 5.8 mm layer of 1 oz carries 8.5 A at 10 K by IPC-2221 external (I = 0.048 × 10^0.44 × 314^0.725, 314 square mils), a rise of about 3 K. `met:` for the OUT_C single-layer stretch, as the board stands after the workmanship pass of 2026-11-19 (commit `Critical routing pass 2`): the front-layer OUT_C copper now ends at the finger to U911 pin 6 (y 44.4 to 46.4); the strip below it (x 41.55 to 46.55, y 47.75 to 69.6), which was joined to nothing at its lower end and carried no current, is deleted. OUT_C therefore runs on B.Cu alone for 47.5 mm, from RV911 pad 1 (y 40.0) to the blade J911 (y 87.5), against the row's 11.05 mm single-layer stretch, which the row's relaxation R1 and the owner's list now state plainly. The back-layer path is 5.0 mm wide (x 41.55 to 46.55) and 4.7 mm at its narrowest, the diagonal below the triac pin (widened from 3.5 mm by the same pass); one 4.7 mm layer of 1 oz carries OUT_C's 4.80 A at about 3.8 K by IPC-2221 external (255 square mils, 4.80 A = 0.048 x dT^0.44 x 255^0.725), and 5.0 mm at about 3.5 K, so the worst section of the run is 3.8 K (about 4.7 milliohm, 0.11 W over 47.5 mm), the row's own test, and the front copper is not joined to the back layer except at T911 pin 2 and RV911 pad 1 (no Mains via, BOARD-16), so no heater current flows in it |
| 1.2 | POWER-3 | function | No via of the standard size on any layer change of a heavy net: RET (10.18 A) needs 51, OUT_S (5.38 A) 27, OUT_C (4.80 A) 24; the layer changes are plated pad barrels (T911, T921, RV911, RV921, J911, J921, J941) and the strips overlapped on both layers; `facts.txt` shows 0 vias on every Mains net. Views `net-_RET-r1c1` to `-r4c1`, `net-_Steam_heater_switch_OUT_S-r1c1` to `-r4c1`, `net-_Coffee_heater_switch_OUT_C-r1c1` to `-r4c1`. The plan row states this as relaxation R2. | no: counting plated pad barrels and overlapped same-net zones as layer changes needs judgement on the views | POWER-3 (it states it) | Catalogue item POWER-3: it counts vias only and gives no reading for plated pad barrels or for strips joined along their whole length on two layers, so the plan row could not meet it as written (amendment commit: 2560a35e) |`overridden by: BOARD-16`. No Mains-class net may carry a via (BOARD-16, safety: a mains via would put mains copper on the back layer beside the low-voltage side), so the heavy nets change layer only in the plated barrels of the through-hole pads they join (two 1.4 mm holes per blade, one 1.1 mm hole per triac pin, the varistor leads) and lie on both layers along their length except the single-layer stretches of BOARD-4. This is the plan's relaxation R2, on the owner's list. |
| 1.3 | POWER-5 | function | U911 and U921 are joined on F.Cu only: pins 6 (OUT_C, OUT_S) to a zone finger and to the 2.1 mm spur and a finger, pins 4 (HEAT_C_OPTO_MT, HEAT_S_OPTO_MT) by bare 0.5 mm tracks, nothing on B.Cu; places (58.1, 45.4), (63.2, 45.4), (58.1, 71.0), (63.2, 71.0). ## Round 2

- **Board judged**: d7fe8e5e, the commit holding the design files as they stand (head of the wall board branch, "Staircase logo and maker line on the front silk"). Judged by an independent reviewer that had not seen the board, from the design files, the checklist (the catalogue's 71 items, MFG-11 being new since round 1, and BOARD-1 to BOARD-18 of `layout-plan.md`), the views, the placement view and the pre-order check's report. Round 1 and its closures were read as history, not as evidence.
- **Views**: rendered from a detached worktree of the commit outside the repository, with `KICAD_CLI=/Applications/KiCad/KiCad.app/Contents/MacOS/kicad-cli`; the classes are the critical classes routing locked:

```
(cd <scratch>/lr2/hardware/sunbeam-em7000/heater-wall-board && task hw:views -- --board heater-wall-board.kicad_pcb --project heater-wall-board.kicad_pro --class Mains --class MainsTrunk --class MainsBranch --out <scratch>/hwb-views-2)
(cd <scratch>/lr2/hardware/sunbeam-em7000/heater-wall-board && task hw:placement -- --board heater-wall-board.kicad_pcb --project heater-wall-board.kicad_pro --out <scratch>/hwb-placement-2)
```

  The views command wrote 77 images, their index and the fact table (30 px/mm, tiles of 4 rows by 1 column for the whole-board copper, class and net views, 3 rows for the other layers); the placement command wrote 4 images and `measures.txt` (LENGTH 373.5906 mm, CROSSINGS 7, OUTSIDE 0, the same as round 1). Every verdict below was judged on the tile images themselves, and the front silkscreen, both copper layers, the assembly, 3D and placement views were each looked at tile by tile. No further view was rendered.
- **Pre-order check**: the whole-repository check, `task hw:verify` (all seven designs, in one run, from the detached worktree), ended `RESULT | PASS | all 7 designs passed` with 0 `VIOLATION` lines in the whole report. The wall board's `DESIGN` line and every `FIGURE` line under it (there is no `VIOLATION` line; 28 `ACCEPTED` lines, 24 `spacing_exception` and 4 `derating_exception`, are the owner-signed exceptions of the board's own separation basis):

### Plan and free pass

**Plan against layout.** The floorplan holds: the four mains rectangles and two low-voltage rectangles are drawn as rule areas at the plan bounds on both copper layers; `measures.txt` OUTSIDE 0; the connector table holds; the outline is the plan's rectangle (20.0, 21.0) to (65.0, 96.0), 45.0 x 75.0 mm, with the two 1.5 mm slots open at the right edge. The `yes` rows and the board's own items were read against the views, each by its verdict row. Departures from the plan rows as written are: RET is 5.8 mm where BOARD-4 and the plan's choices say 6.0 mm, OUT_C runs on B.Cu alone for 47.5 mm where the BOARD-4 row lists 11.05 mm (finding 1.1's closure, and finding 2.3 here), the one via is the +5V_WB via and not an OUT_S one (as round 1 recorded), and the plan holds no row for MFG-11, which the catalogue gained from round 1's finding 1.10 (finding 2.2).

**Every closure of round 1 re-tested against the board as it stands.** 1.1: the arithmetic holds (255 square mils at 4.80 A gives 3.8 K, 271 square mils 3.5 K, 314 square mils at 5.09 A a layer about 3 K, 4.7 mm measured on the back diagonal as the perpendicular distance between its parallel edges), but the closure says the row's relaxation R1 and the owner's list "now state plainly" the 47.5 mm single-layer run and they do not (finding 2.3); its "about 4 K for the whole run" is not a figure the arithmetic gives (the worst section is 3.8 K). 1.2, 1.3, 1.4, 1.7, 1.8: the reasons still apply, the copper they rest on is unchanged. 1.5 and 1.6: the changes are in the project file and the rules table as stated (`min_hole_clearance` 0.35, `min_via_annular_width` 0.20 with its `below_recommended` entry, the `Ground pad reliefs` row). 1.9: six rectangles on F.Fab at the listed coordinates. 1.10: the legend was drawn, and is judged under the new item MFG-11 (finding 2.1).

**Free pass.** Seen on the views and not asked by any item: the front silk hazard block (triangle x 38.0 to 44.0, y 50.9 to 56.1, text to y 65.6) stands over the 0.5 mm HEAT_S_GATE_R track at x 38.6 (finding 2.4); the three pin names of J931 are detached from the header (finding 2.1); each opto-triac has two pin-1 dots, one the footprint's inside the body outline at the pin-1 corner and one added outside the pad, both at the same corner (no finding: they agree); the right-hand text block ends 0.8 mm inside the right edge and the logo stands 0.35 mm from the front OUT_C block's edge, both on mask-covered laminate with no check line; no hook, loop, stub or via thicket; the through-hole drill marks drawn on every layer are the views' own marking. In the copper the workmanship review of 2026-11-19 recorded, nothing further was found: the right edge of the back OUT_C copper stands 2.5 mm in, the diagonal measures 4.7 mm, the front OUT_C copper ends at the U911 finger, the RET corner is cut at 45 degrees. In the records (repository-wide sweeps with several phrasings: the OUT_C extents, 3.5 and 4.7 mm, 1.0 and 2.5 mm from the edge, no fiducials, part counts, MOC3083SR2M and MOC3083SVM, about 3.5 K and about 4 K, the silk legend contents): the diagonal, the edge distance, the front OUT_C extent, the K figures, the fiducials and the opto-triac part name are consistent (MOC3083SR2M survives only in the footprint's file name, which the sweep log keeps on purpose, and in history). Not consistent: the outline length and several statements, findings 2.6 to 2.8. The record itself has three faults, finding 2.9. Not judged and why: `hw:review-history` needs the record committed (skipped); the fetch of the maker's capability page was not repeated (annular row not in the page's server text in round 1; the figure rests on the board's row).

**Independent workmanship review of 2026-11-19, and copper pass 2 (recorded after round 1 closed, moved here from inside round 1).**

After the closing of round 1 an independent blind workmanship review of the board was run on the committed design files by a reviewer that had not seen this record. It scored the seven areas 7, 8, 7, 7, 8, 8 and 7 out of 10, overall acceptable to good, with no MUST finding. Its findings and the designer's rulings:

- SHOULD-1, the back-layer OUT_C copper to the right of the current path, 1.0 mm from the board edge: accepted. The right edge was moved in from x 64.0 to x 62.5 (2.5 mm from the edge) and the widening from the triac pin made one 45 degree line. The second place it named, the OUT_S bar at the right edge by U921: rejected, since it reaches no nearer the edge (1.05 mm) than U921's own pin 6 pad (1.07 mm), whose place the low-voltage edge fixes.
- SHOULD-2, the front OUT_C strip that joins nothing and makes the board look as if OUT_C ran on two layers: accepted by deleting the dead front copper (y 47.75 to 69.6); the option of plated holes joining the layers was rejected, since they are mains vias that BOARD-16 forbids.
- SHOULD-3, the blade backing: not a copper matter and not rejected; it is a case requirement, recorded in `open-items.md` (blade backing).
- COULD, the top-left corner of the RET strip: accepted, cut at 45 degrees, 3.5 mm, on both layers. COULD, the silk hazard block: accepted, moved to x 41.0 with MAINS and VOLTAGE on two lines (it stood over one 0.5 mm gate track under mask; round 2, finding 2.4, moved it again to x 44.75).
- Rejected with reasons: the lone front block under J911 (joined to the back by the blade's two barrels, so the blade is soldered to copper on both faces); narrowing the OUT_S feed bar to 1 mm (a MainsBranch track, whose rule holds 2.05 mm); moving RV911 (placement is committed and it stands 1.2 mm outside the T911 to U911 air path); moving the no-coat note (assembly layer, not printed).
- The designer's own finding in the same pass: the back-layer OUT_C diagonal was 3.5 mm wide, not the 5.0 mm BOARD-4 states; it is now 4.7 mm, which keeps the 10.70 mm distance from back-layer mains copper to the low-voltage pads that 5.0 mm would have shortened.

Four locked zones changed (OUT_C front and back, RET front and back); no track, via, pad or part moved, no placement changed. The locked-copper compare of the revised board against the record of the committed board reported exactly those four zones as CHANGED and the other 17 items unchanged; the record was then taken again and the compare passes with 21 items. The KiCad rule check and the pre-order check report the same lines as before (12 clearance, 12 creepage, 42 spacing_exception, 4 mains_declaration for the wall board).

**Closing round.** The silk change of commit `7588acbda0182d8c19debf06ab2cf0f7651d401f` changed the board's design files after round 2's Board judged commit, so round 2 is no longer the closing round on unchanged files: a round 3, judged on the committed board, is needed before the review can close.

## Round 3

- **Board judged**: 53cee6f9 (HEAD of the wall board branch; the design files are those of `7588acbda0182d8c19debf06ab2cf0f7651d401f`, the silk change after round 2's Board judged commit d7fe8e5e; the commits after it, 5d0ffc62, ab5c1cfe and 53cee6f9, change records only). Judged by an independent reviewer that had not seen the board, from the design files, the checklist (the catalogue's 71 items and BOARD-1 to BOARD-18 of `layout-plan.md`, 89 rows), the views, the placement view and the pre-order check's report. Rounds 1 and 2 were read as history, not as evidence; every verdict below was judged on the views of this round, and a verdict that cites a finding of an earlier round does so only after the board's copper was seen to be unchanged and the closure's reason still to apply.
- **Views**: rendered from a detached worktree of 53cee6f9 outside the repository, with `KICAD_CLI=/Applications/KiCad/KiCad.app/Contents/MacOS/kicad-cli`; the classes are the critical classes routing locked:

```
(cd <scratch>/lr3/hardware/sunbeam-em7000/heater-wall-board && task hw:views -- --board heater-wall-board.kicad_pcb --project heater-wall-board.kicad_pro --class Mains --class MainsTrunk --class MainsBranch --out <scratch>/hwb-views-3)
(cd <scratch>/lr3/hardware/sunbeam-em7000/heater-wall-board && task hw:placement -- --board heater-wall-board.kicad_pcb --project heater-wall-board.kicad_pro --out <scratch>/hwb-placement-3)
```

  The views command wrote 77 images, their index and the fact table (30 px/mm, the same set as round 2); the placement command wrote 4 images and `measures.txt` (LENGTH 373.5906 mm, CROSSINGS 7, OUTSIDE 0, the same as rounds 1 and 2). The front and back silkscreen, both copper layers, the edge, the assembly top, the placement, the class map, the 3D views, `crop-U921` and the tiles of RET and OUT_S were each looked at as images. One further check was run, not a view: the KiCad rule check on the board as committed (`kicad-cli pcb drc`, 12 clearance lines, the accepted pairs) and the same check on a scratch copy of the board with the maker-mark text height set to 0.5 mm, to see whether KiCad reports a text below the text-height minimum (it did not; finding 3.2).
- **Pre-order check**: the whole-repository check, `python3 hardware/tools/board_check.py --root .` (the command `task hw:verify` runs), all seven designs in one run from the detached worktree, ended `RESULT | PASS | all 7 designs passed` with 0 `VIOLATION` lines in the whole report (7 `DESIGN` lines, all PASS; 319 `ACCEPTED` lines, 14 `derating_exception`, 88 `spacing_exception` and 217 `workmanship_acceptance`; 265 warnings, all main board). ### Plan and free pass

**Plan against layout.** The floorplan holds: the four mains and two low-voltage rectangles are drawn as rule areas on both copper layers; OUTSIDE 0; the connector table holds (J941, J921, J911 on the bottom edge, J931 pin 1 at (63.18, 58.20)); the outline is (20.0, 21.0) to (65.0, 96.0), 45.0 by 75.0 mm, with the two 1.5 mm slots open at the right edge. The plan's `yes` rows and the board's own items were read against the views, each by its verdict row. The plan's MFG-11 row, which lists the legends, is judged under that item (findings 3.1 and 3.2); its statements that the pin names and `TO MAIN BOARD J761` are at J931 are the plan row as written and the layout does not meet them (finding 3.1). Departures that earlier rounds recorded and the plan's amendment notes carry (RET 5.8 mm, OUT_C 47.5 mm on B.Cu alone, the one +5V_WB via) were read against the board and stand.

**Every closure of an earlier round re-tested against the board as it stands.** The heavy copper is unchanged since round 2 (the zone polygons of OUT_C were read from the board file: the back diagonal edges are the lines x+y = 89.3 and x+y = 95.9, 4.69 mm apart perpendicular, the 5.0 mm strips at x 41.55 to 46.55 and x 56.45 to 62.45, the front OUT_C copper ends at y 46.425), so the reasons of 1.1, 1.2, 1.3, 1.4, 1.7 and 1.8 still apply and the corresponding fail rows cite them. 1.1's arithmetic holds: 255 square mils at 4.80 A gives 3.8 K by the IPC-2221 external formula, as the plan's R1 and the owner's list now state. 1.5 and 1.6: the rows are in `design-rules.md` (copper to hole 0.35 mm, annular 0.20 mm, `Ground pad reliefs`). 1.9: six dashed rectangles on F.Fab. 1.10, 2.1 and 2.4 closed by the silk change: re-tested in finding 3.1 below, 2.4's closure holds (the hazard block stands at x 39.6 to 49.9 on bare laminate, 1.0 mm from the HEAT_S_GATE_R track at x 38.6 and clear of every pad); 2.2 to 2.9 closed by record changes, re-tested by the sweeps below, which found one new stale statement (finding 3.3).

**Free pass.** Seen on the views and not asked by any item: the pin-1 dots of U911 and U921 come in pairs at the same corner (round 2 saw the same, they agree); the back carries the mirrored order-number placeholder at x 51.5 to 61.6, y 79.5 to 80.5 on bare laminate with no copper beneath it on either side and no pad or mask opening, and no board name or hazard mark on the back, the face against the case tray and the plate in service; the front hazard block, the pin-name columns and `TO MAIN BOARD J761` all stand in the strip between the mains rectangle and the low-voltage pocket and over bare laminate; the printed `TO MAIN BOARD J761` ends 0.4 mm from the courtyard of U921 and about 1 mm above the reference R921 (no overlap with anything). In the records (repository-wide sweeps over `hardware/sunbeam-em7000` and `docs`, several phrasings each: `beside J931`, `beside the header`, `no back-side legend`, `back face carries no`, `back carries no`, `no text on B.SilkS`, `no legend`, `pin names`, `x 41.0`, `x 47.4`, `moved to x 41`, `bare laminate`, `45 x 76`, `76.0`, `47.5 mm`, `4.7 mm`, `3.8 K`, `about 4 K`, `about 3.5 K`, `11.05`, `45 x 75`, `hazard`, `JLC`): the outline figure, the K figures, the 47.5 mm and 4.7 mm statements, the hazard block's place (x 44.75), the pin-name x positions (52.6, 54.1, 55.6), the placeholder (56.5, 80.0) and the maker mark (50.6, 90.55 and 92.25) agree with the board file; the plan's MFG-7 and MFG-8 rows ("the back face carries no text") are baseline rows with an amendment note saying so, and `sweep-log.md` records the others. Not consistent: the pin-name text's stated y position, finding 3.3. The records also say the maker-mark text is on the board without a height, so none states the 0.9 mm (finding 3.2). Not judged and why: `hw:review-history` was not run (known failure on round 1's first recording, accepted and disclosed by the owner); the fetch of the maker's capability page was not repeated; the `PinCoverageProof` test failure on origin/main is not this board's.

| Finding | Raised by | Severity | What and where | Rule | Added as | Planning shortfall | Closed by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3.1 | MFG-11 | quality (neatness) | The J931 legend does not read plainly as J931's. (a) The three pin-name columns (`3 HEAT_S_LED_K` at x 52.6, `2 HEAT_C_LED_K` at x 54.1, `1 +5V_WB` at x 55.6, F.SilkS, reading upward, y 52.4 to 64.4) stand 5.5 to 7.6 mm left of their own header pads at a 1.5 mm pitch where the pads are 2.54 mm apart (pads x 58.10, 60.64 and 63.18, pin 1 the square pad at the right), so no name stands over its own pin: the nearest pad to the label `1 +5V_WB` is pin 3, and the foot of the column `1 +5V_WB` (x 55.6, y about 63.9) is under 1 mm from the courtyard of U921, whose pads are mains, and about 9.5 mm from J931 pin 1; the order 3, 2, 1 left to right is right and the numbers are right (checked against the board's J931 pad nets and the schematic sheet), but a person has only the numbers and the footprint pin-1 bracket to go by. (b) `TO MAIN BOARD J761` (1.0 mm, right-justified at x 56.2, y 68.25, spanning x 40.0 to 56.2) stands 10 mm below the header and directly below the hazard block's last line `230 V~ 50 Hz` at the same size, 0.4 mm from U921's courtyard and about 1 mm above the reference R921, so it reads as the hazard block's caption or U921's, and the item fails a legend that stands nearer another part than its own. Views `layer-F.SilkS-r2c1`, `layer-F.SilkS-r3c1`, `assembly-top-r3c1`, `crop-U921`, `3d-top` (board x 39 to 57, y 52 to 69). ## Round 4

- **Board judged**: ef640847 (HEAD of the wall board branch). The design files are those of `60c665fc312cd7b7399ede4f57c4177da3499b14`, the silk change that closed findings 3.1 to 3.3; the commit after it, ef640847, changes records only (`git diff 60c665fc ef640847` touches `design-rules.md`, `fabrication-notes.md`, `layout-review.md` and `sweep-log.md`, no KiCad file). Since round 3's judged files (7588acbd) the board file differs only in silk text and silk graphics (the diff holds no segment, via, zone or pad line), so the copper, the edge and the placement are those rounds 1 to 3 judged. Judged by an independent reviewer that had not seen the board, from the design files, the checklist (the catalogue's 71 items and BOARD-1 to BOARD-18 of `layout-plan.md`, 89 rows), the views, the placement view and the pre-order check's report. Rounds 1 to 3 were read as history, not as evidence: every verdict below was judged on this round's views, and a verdict that cites an earlier finding does so only after the board's copper was seen unchanged and the closure's reason still to apply.
- **Views**: rendered from a detached worktree of ef640847 outside the repository, with `KICAD_CLI=/Applications/KiCad/KiCad.app/Contents/MacOS/kicad-cli`; the classes are the critical classes routing locked:

```
(cd <scratch>/lr4/hardware/sunbeam-em7000/heater-wall-board && task hw:views -- --board heater-wall-board.kicad_pcb --project heater-wall-board.kicad_pro --class Mains --class MainsTrunk --class MainsBranch --out <scratch>/v4)
(cd <scratch>/lr4/hardware/sunbeam-em7000/heater-wall-board && task hw:placement -- --board heater-wall-board.kicad_pcb --project heater-wall-board.kicad_pro --out <scratch>/p4)
```

  The views command wrote 77 images, their index and the fact table (30 px/mm); the placement command wrote 4 images and `measures.txt` (LENGTH 373.5906 mm, CROSSINGS 7, OUTSIDE 0). Looked at as images: the front silkscreen (three tiles), the back silkscreen, both copper layers (four tiles each), the edge, the front fab, the front paste, the assembly of both sides, the class map, the placement, `crop-U911`, `crop-U921`, `3d-top`, `3d-bottom` and `3d-angled`. Net views were read through the copper layers and the class map, and the three tiles of HEAT_S_GATE_R were not opened as separate images. Two further checks that are not views: the KiCad rule check on the board as committed (`kicad-cli pcb drc`, 12 `clearance` lines, the accepted pairs, nothing else), and the maker's capability page fetched live on 2026-11-19 for its Legend and Outline entries.
- **Pre-order check**: the whole-repository check, `python3 hardware/tools/board_check.py --root .` (the command `task hw:verify` runs), all seven designs in one run from the detached worktree, ended `RESULT | PASS | all 7 designs passed` with 0 `VIOLATION` lines in the whole report (7 `DESIGN` lines, all PASS; 319 `ACCEPTED` lines, 14 `derating_exception`, 88 `spacing_exception` and 217 `workmanship_acceptance`; 265 warnings, all main board). The wall board's `DESIGN` line and every `FIGURE` line under it (no `VIOLATION` line; 28 `ACCEPTED` lines, 24 `spacing_exception` and 4 `derating_exception`):

### Plan and free pass

**Plan against layout.** The floorplan holds: the four mains and two low-voltage rectangles are drawn as rule areas on both copper layers at the plan's bounds; OUTSIDE 0; the connector table holds (J941, J921, J911 on the bottom edge, J931 pin 1 at (63.18, 58.20)); the outline is (20.0, 21.0) to (65.0, 96.0), 45.0 by 75.0 mm, with the two 1.5 mm slots open at the right edge; H1 at (61.50, 92.50). The plan's `yes` rows and the board's own items were read against the views, each by its verdict row. The plan's MFG-11 row says the pin names and the cable to J761 stand at J931 as `fabrication-notes.md` "Front silkscreen" states: the layout meets it. Departures the plan's amendment notes carry (RET 5.8 mm, OUT_C 47.5 mm on B.Cu alone, the one +5V_WB via) were read against the board and stand.

**Every closure of an earlier round re-tested against the board as it stands.** The copper is unchanged since the round that closed it (git diff of the board file from 7588acbd to HEAD holds no segment, via, zone or pad line), so the reasons of 1.1, 1.2, 1.3, 1.4, 1.7 and 1.8 still apply and their fail rows cite them. 1.5 and 1.6: the rows are in `design-rules.md`. 1.9: six dashed rectangles on F.Fab, seen. 1.10, 2.1, 2.4 and 3.1: re-tested below. 3.2: a census of the board file gives 23 silk `gr_text` and 15 reference texts, 38 in all, none under 1.0 mm high or 0.15 mm stroke; the maker mark is 1.0 mm. 3.3: every position `fabrication-notes.md` "Front silkscreen" gives (legend rows ending at x 55.55, y 59.7, 61.2, 62.7 and 64.2; J931's reference at (53.7, 58.2); hazard block anchors x 44.45; maker lines at x 50.15, y 89.0 and 90.6; name lines ending at x 64.3; STEAM and COFFEE at x 41.2 and 48.6; the placeholder at (56.5, 80.0)) equals the board file's text anchors.

**The J931 legend, as asked (the choice of a table beside the header is the owner's and is not raised).** Correct. The board's pad nets are pad 1 (square, (63.18, 58.20)) +5V_WB, pad 2 (60.64) HEAT_C_LED_K, pad 3 (58.10) HEAT_S_LED_K, so the pads read 3, 2, 1 from left to right; the table gives `1 +5V_WB`, `2 HEAT_C_LED_K`, `3 HEAT_S_LED_K` and `TO MAIN BOARD J761`, matching the board, the schematic's J931 sheet and `pin-allocation.yaml` (pins 1, 2, 3). It is 1.0 mm text at 0.15 mm stroke, right-aligned to x 55.55, reading left to right, J931's reference level with the header above it; nothing overlaps (the table is 0.78 mm from J931's courtyard, 1.03 mm from U921's for the rows at y 62.7 and 64.2, 0.95 mm for the J761 line, which ends 0.76 mm above slot B's edge). Rows 2 to 4 are nearer U921's courtyard than J931's, which the owner has accepted; they carry the pin number and net name and J931's reference stands over them, so I do not read them as U921's. Pin-1 mark: the square pad at the right-hand end plus a silk bracket at its upper right corner ((63.18, 56.82) to (64.56, 56.82) to (64.56, 58.20)), 0.44 mm inside the edge; no pin number is printed at the other two pads, so the order 3, 2, 1 left to right is learned from the pin-1 end and the table; that is the accepted layout.

**U921's pin-1 dot against the edge.** The dot is a filled circle of centre (64.45, 61.45) and radius 0.25 mm with a 0.10 mm stroke, so its rim is 0.25 mm from the board edge at x 65.0 (0.55 mm centre to edge), 0.22 mm from the pin-1 pad. The maker's capability page, read today, gives no silkscreen-to-edge minimum: its Legend entries are line width 0.15 mm, text height 1.0 mm, ratio 1:6 and pad to silkscreen 0.15 mm, and its Outline entries a regular-precision edge tolerance of plus or minus 0.2 mm and copper clearance of 0.2 mm. A dot that the tolerance nicked would lose a sliver of a mark that is paired with the footprint's own pin-1 dot inside the body corner at (63.64, 63.91) and the pad chamfer on the fab layer, so I do not find a defect in it. U911's dot (56.7, 54.95) is 0.35 mm clear of its pad.

**Free pass.** Seen on the views and not asked by any item: the via of +5V_WB at (58.10, 56.80) lies on the top line of J931's silk outline (y 56.82); the board tents vias on both faces (`(tenting (front yes) (back yes))`), so silk over a mask-covered via is no manufacturing fault. The back carries the maker's order-number placeholder `JLCJLCJLCJLC` at (56.5, 80.0) on bare laminate with no copper beneath and no hazard mark or name (the face against the case tray); the front board name stands over it on the other face, which is no collision. The front hazard block (triangle apex (44.45, 43.7), text y 50.4 to 56.45) stands on laminate with no F.Cu copper under it (the OUT_C strip runs on B.Cu there). The silk dots at the opto-triacs come in pairs at the pin-1 corner, which agree. Two statements and one measure are findings: finding 4.1 (a record) and finding 4.2 (silk strokes).

**Stale statements.** Repository sweeps over `hardware/sunbeam-em7000` and `docs`, several phrasings each: `45 x 76`, `76 mm`, `76.0 mm`, `45 by 76` (only the frozen plan baseline row WORK-9 with its amendment note, and `sweep-log.md`); `44.75`, `x 41.0`, `x 47.4`, `y 58.6` (only `sweep-log.md` and frozen round text); `52.6`, `54.1`, `55.6` (frozen round 2 text); `scale 0.45`, `0.45 scale`, `0.9 mm` (none live for the board); `y 64.6`; `beside J931`, `beside the header`, `TO MAIN BOARD` (the notes state the table as drawn); `no back-side`, `back carries no`, `back face carries no`, `no text on B.SilkS`, `no legend` (the plan's baseline rows MFG-7 and MFG-8 carry an amendment note; `fabrication-notes.md` states the placeholder); `JLCJLC`, `order number`; `47.5 mm`, `4.7 mm`, `3.8 K`, `about 4 K` (agree with the board); `fiducial` (every record says none, by decision D2, and `design-rules.md` has its row); `MOC3083SVM` against `SR2M` (the footprint name is explained in `fabrication-notes.md`; the sheets, `schematic-spec.yaml`, `parts-specification.yaml` and `separation-basis.yaml` agree) and `DIP-6`, `through-hole`, `M part`: one live stale sentence, finding 4.1. The silk text positions, the 38-text census and the 45.0 by 75.0 outline agree with the board file. Not judged and why: `hw:review-history` was not run (known failure on round 1's first recording, accepted and disclosed by the owner); the `PinCoverageProof` test failure on origin/main is not this board's; the maker's page was read again only for its Legend and Outline entries; silk text height has no measured rule yet (carried, owed to the method and check work), and finding 4.2 adds the stroke width of silk graphics to that carry.

### Plan and free pass

**Plan against layout.** The floorplan holds: the four mains and two low-voltage rectangles are drawn as rule areas on both copper layers at the plan's bounds (the F.Cu polygons read from the board file equal the rows of `design-rules.md`); OUTSIDE 0; the connector table holds (J941, J921 and J911 at x 24.50, 32.60 and 44.00, y 90.04; J931 pin 1 at (63.18, 58.20), read from the board file); the outline is (20.0, 21.0) to (65.0, 96.0), 45.0 by 75.0 mm, with the two 1.5 mm slots open at the right edge; H1 at (61.50, 92.50). The plan's `yes` rows and the board's own items were read against the views, each by its verdict row. The departures the plan's amendment notes carry were read against the board and stand: RET 5.8 mm, OUT_C on B.Cu alone for 47.5 mm from y 40.0 to y 87.5 and 4.7 mm at its narrowest (measured here from the zone polygon: the diagonal edges (51.65, 37.6) to (41.55, 47.75) and (53.4, 42.5) to (46.55, 49.35) are 4.70 mm apart), about 3.8 K at 4.80 A by the IPC-2221 external curve for 4.7 mm of 35 um copper (255 square mils; 3.8 K recomputed), and the one +5V_WB via.

**Free pass.** Seen on the views and not asked by any item: the +5V_WB via at (58.10, 56.80) lies on the top line of J931's silk outline, which is no manufacturing fault since the board tents its vias; the back carries only the maker's order-number placeholder, over bare laminate; the hazard block stands on laminate with no F.Cu copper under it; the opto-triacs' silk dots come in pairs at the pin-1 corner; the maker mark stands 0.8 mm from J911's courtyard and clear of every pad. The records were read against the board and against each other. Everything below agrees with the board: the 45.0 by 75.0 outline (`45 x 76`, `76 mm`, `45 by 76` live only in the frozen plan baseline row WORK-9 with its amendment note), the fiducial statements (every record says none, by decision D2, and `design-rules.md` has its row), MOC3083SVM against SR2M and C23552 or C111561 (each live statement carries its 2026-11-19 amendment; the footprint name is explained in `fabrication-notes.md`), the 47.5 mm, 4.7 mm and about 3.8 K figures, the silk positions and the 38-text census, the J931 table as drawn, the 96.4, 111.4, 123.8 and 138.8 degC junction figures in `circuit-proof.md`, `constraints-figures.md` and the controller boards' constraints record, the laminate documents (the Shengyi S1000-2M and S1000-2 sheets with their revision, page and distributor-hosted copy) and the written request to the manufacturer, which `fabrication-notes.md` states as a plan: no record in the repository shows it sent, it is the owner's pre-order act and stays to be made, and C11 is stated as met on paper with the shipped laminate not verified; and the signed basis, which every wall-board record now says is this board's own `separation-basis.yaml`, signed by the owner on 2026-11-19 (W-12) while the shared basis is not edited. Not agreeing: the 45 degC working figure, which the amendments of 2026-11-19 and 2026-11-20 supersede for the triacs' wall path (25 degC nominal and 40 degC bound, with 70 degC governing the board-side parts), is still stated as the live basis, with no amendment note, in `docs/sunbeam-em7000/controller-board-function-forms.md` (line 287, the opto-triac range margin) and in `docs/sunbeam-em7000/controller-board-division.md` (line 24, the decision row; line 192, the premises row for the air temperature; line 1168), although `sweep-log.md` records the 2026-11-20 sweep of `docs/sunbeam-em7000/` as leaving only history labelled superseded: finding 5.1. Not judged and why: `hw:review-history` was not run (known failure, round 1's first recording and round 3's order, accepted and disclosed by the owner); the `PinCoverageProof` test failure on origin/main is not this board's; silk text height and stroke width have no measured rule yet, carried by `SOL-PRE-ORDER-CHECK-MEASURES-SILK-TEXT-HEIGHT-AND-STROKE-WIDTH`.

### Plan and free pass

**Plan against layout.** The floorplan holds: the four mains and two low-voltage rectangles are drawn as rule areas on both copper layers at the plan's bounds (read from the board file, equal to the rows of `design-rules.md`); OUTSIDE 0; the connector table holds (J941, J921 and J911 at x 24.50, 32.60 and 44.00, y 90.04; J931 pin 1 at (63.18, 58.20), read from the board file); the outline is (20.0, 21.0) to (65.0, 96.0), 45.0 by 75.0 mm, with the two 1.5 mm slots open at the right edge; H1 at (61.50, 92.50). The plan's `yes` rows and the board's own items were read against the views, each by its verdict row. The departures the plan's amendment notes carry were read against the board and stand: RET 5.8 mm; OUT_C on the back layer alone for 47.5 mm from RV911 pad 1 (y 40.0) to the blade, 4.7 mm at its narrowest (the diagonal edges (51.65, 37.6) to (41.55, 47.75) and (53.4, 42.5) to (46.55, 49.35) are 4.70 mm apart), about 3.8 K at 4.80 A (255 square mils of 35 um copper, IPC-2221 external, recomputed here as 3.80 K); the one +5V_WB via.

**What changed since round 5, read from the files.** Only the silk strokes, as the opening lines state. The design-rules row `Silkscreen line width` and the sweep log give a census of 118 printed strokes, none under 0.15 mm (back face 1 text; front face 79 graphics at 0.15 mm, 1 at 0.25 mm, 29 texts at 0.15 mm and 8 at 0.2 mm); a census made here from the board file, texts, reference properties and graphics, inside the placed footprints and on the board, on both faces, finds the same: 80 graphics (79 at 0.15 mm, the hazard triangle at 0.25 mm), 38 texts (30 at 0.15 mm, 8 at 0.2 mm, every one 1.0 mm high or more), 15 hidden Value properties that print nothing. The 29 and 8 strokes below 0.15 mm of the board file correspond to the 50 the maker's production-files check counted segment by segment (12 at 0.10 mm and 38 at 0.12 mm: a rectangle and a polygon are several segments each), so the two counts agree and are not two figures for one thing; no record states the 50.

**The silkscreen at 0.15 mm, looked at.** The hazard triangle (stroke 0.25 mm) with its filled bolt (0.15 mm) reads cleanly at 30 px/mm; the bolt's lower point, (44.0, 48.96), stands inside the triangle's base stroke (y 48.77 to 49.02), so the bolt touches the base: that is as drawn at 0.10 mm too (the points did not change; the outline grew 0.025 mm each side), it reads as the usual high-voltage mark, and it is not raised. The seven pin-1 dots are filled discs of 0.6 mm across, equal to each other and to what they printed before: four at the opto-triacs (the footprint dots inside the body corner, near (57.6, 52.5) and (63.64, 63.91), and the board dots at (56.7, 54.95) and (64.45, 61.45)) and three under the blades (x 24.5, 32.6 and 44.0, y 95.04). The triac outlines (three sides and the pin-1 tick, 0.15 mm), the three blade rectangles, the 1206 end ticks, the opto-triac body lines, the J931 outline with its pin-1 bracket and the varistor outlines are plain continuous lines; none touches a pad. The J931 legend table, the blade names, the board name and the maker mark read in order; their positions equal the board file's anchors and `fabrication-notes.md` (legend rows ending at x 55.55, y 59.7, 61.2, 62.7 and 64.2; J931's reference at (53.7, 58.2); the hazard block at x 44.45 with DANGER at y 50.4, MAINS 52.5, VOLTAGE 54.6 and the 230 V line at 56.45; the maker lines at x 50.15, y 89.0 and 90.6; the name lines ending at x 64.3 at y 80.3, 82.3, 83.9 and 86.0; STEAM and COFFEE at x 41.2 and 48.6, y 25.6; the placeholder at (56.5, 80.0)); the 38-text census agrees.

**U921's pin-1 dot against the edge.** The dot is a filled circle of centre (64.45, 61.45) and, at 0.15 mm, radius 0.225 mm: its printed rim is at x 64.75, 0.25 mm from the board edge's centre line at x 65.0 (0.55 mm centre to edge). The 0.25 mm of round 4 is that figure. The 0.203 mm figure reported for the same dot is the distance to the drawn edge's inner face, since the Edge.Cuts line is 0.10 mm wide (the face at x 64.95 is 0.20 mm from the rim, and 0.203 mm is that to the raster's precision; the board file allows no other reading): the two figures measure from the centre line and from the face, and neither is wrong. It does not matter to the maker's limits: the capability page read today states no silkscreen to edge minimum (Legend: line width 0.15 mm, text height 1.0 mm, ratio 1:6, pad to silkscreen 0.15 mm; Outline: copper clearance from routed edges 0.2 mm, edge tolerance plus or minus 0.2 mm), the dot has no copper under it, and a nick from the edge tolerance would take a sliver off a mark that is paired with the footprint's own dot at (63.64, 63.91) and the chamfer on the fab layer.

**Every closure of an earlier round re-tested against the board as it stands.** The copper is unchanged since round 3, so the reasons of 1.1, 1.2, 1.3, 1.4, 1.7 and 1.8 still apply and their fail rows cite them (POWER-2, POWER-3, POWER-5, CONN-4, WORK-1, BOARD-4 and BOARD-5; H1 measures 14.95 mm from the nearest Mains copper, so 1.4's `overridden by: BOARD-11` holds). 1.5 and 1.6: the rows are in `design-rules.md`. 1.9: six dashed rectangles on F.Fab, seen. 1.10, 2.1, 2.4, 3.1 to 3.3: the silk anchors equal the records (above). 2.3: relaxation R1 states the 47.5 mm run, 4.7 mm and 3.8 K, and the owner list has it as item 5. 4.1: the through-hole sentences are gone. 5.1: read on the four lines of `docs/sunbeam-em7000` (function-forms 287, division 24, 192 and 1168), each carries its `Amended 2026-11-20` note; the sweep over `45 degC` and `45 °C` finds only amended or superseded history. 4.2: its reason, that the 29 strokes at 0.12 mm are accepted, no longer applies, since the board no longer has them, and MFG-1's silk row now passes on the board; the record's Closed by for 4.2 still says the exception stands and is raised as finding 6.1.

**Stale statements.** Sweeps over `hardware/sunbeam-em7000` and `docs/sunbeam-em7000`, several phrasings each, `layout-review.md`, `schematic-review.md` and `sweep-log.md` read as history: stroke widths `0.10`, `0.12`, `0.15` with silk, stroke, legend or line width (the design-rules row, the fabrication notes and the sweep log state the census and the exception's end; one live statement is inexact, finding 6.2; the exception stands only in frozen round 4 and 5 text and in 4.2's Closed by, finding 6.1); the counts 29, 37, 50 and 118 (all agree with the board file, above); `45 x 76`, `76 mm` (only the frozen plan baseline row WORK-9 with its amendment note; the other hits are 0.76 mm); the 45.0 by 75.0 outline (board file, plan, fabrication notes and constraints agree); `MOC3083SVM` against `MOC3083SR2M`, `MOC3083M`, `C899868`, `C111561` and `C23552` (the sheets, `schematic-spec.yaml`, `parts-specification.yaml`, `separation-basis.yaml`, the board's LCSC fields (C899868 on U911 and U921) and the footprint name explained in `fabrication-notes.md` agree; the library symbol keeps the family name MOC3083M); `47.5`, `4.7` and `3.8 K` (agree with the board); silk positions (above); the laminate documents (the Shengyi S1000-2M and S1000-2 sheets with revision, page and distributor-hosted copy) and the written request to the maker, which `fabrication-notes.md` and owner item 6 state as a plan, not yet sent, with no record showing it sent; W-12 (board-own basis signed 2026-11-19), W-13 and W-14 (the entry counts, 17 entries and 14 in his words, agree; W-14's 7 mm re-confirmation is carried into `constraints-figures.md`, `open-items.md` and `layout-hedges.md`). One statement that is history and not raised: W-13 and `owner-sign-off.md` line 21 record the owner's acceptance of closing the review at round 5 with no further round; the silk change of `8ee00b84` came after it, so the review closes at this round and not at round 5, which this record states and which the owner may wish to confirm.

**Free pass.** Seen on the views and not asked by any item: the +5V_WB via at (58.10, 56.80) lies on the top line of J931's silk outline, which is no manufacturing fault since the board tents its vias; the back carries only the maker's order-number placeholder, over bare laminate; the hazard block stands on laminate with no F.Cu copper under it; the maker mark stands clear of every pad; the opto-triacs' silk dots come in pairs at the pin-1 corner, which agree. The records were read against the board and against each other. Two statements are findings: 6.1 and 6.2. Not judged and why: `hw:review-history` was not run, since it refuses a record that differs from HEAD and this draft is not committed; its four disclosed failures (round 1 first recording, round 3 order, round 5 structure and first recording) are known, accepted and not raised again, as are the `PinCoverageProof` test failure on origin/main, the J931 table legend, the 0.95 mm distance of the TO MAIN BOARD row, the 0.20 mm triac annulus and the missing measured rule for silk text height and stroke width (`SOL-PRE-ORDER-CHECK-MEASURES-SILK-TEXT-HEIGHT-AND-STROKE-WIDTH`).

## Round 7

- **Board judged**: 9e6bb8eec2681c75d0f6103d7a301b12f5bd5e2c (HEAD of the wall board branch, read in a detached worktree outside the repository). The design files are those of `361c0406d74d51ffa5be3352206ef0f96b5031ea`, "critical routing pass 3": `git diff --name-only d2c1f97e 9e6bb8ee -- hardware/sunbeam-em7000/heater-wall-board` lists the records `design-rules.md`, `fabrication-notes.md`, `layout-review.md`, `owner-sign-off.md` and `sweep-log.md` and one KiCad file, `heater-wall-board.kicad_pcb`; no sheet, no project file (`heater-wall-board.kicad_pro`) and nothing in `heater-wall-board.pretty` differs. Against the board round 6 judged (`d2c1f97e`, design files of `8ee00b84`; end-of-round commit `bd942f31`) `git diff bd942f31 9e6bb8ee` of `heater-wall-board.kicad_pcb` was read in full here, not taken from the commit message, and holds only these blocks: (a) the front OUT_C zone `OUT_C_F0` (locked): the outline's vertices `(46.55, 42.75) (46.55, 46.425) (58.1, 46.425) (58.1, 44.425) (49.875, 44.425) (58.4, 35.9)` become `(49.3, 40) (49.3, 46.425) (58.1, 46.425) (58.1, 36.2) (58.4, 35.9)`, with the filled polygon refilled to match; (b) the `HEAT_S_OPTO_MT` run from R921 pad 1 to U921 pin 4: the track `(52.965, 72.5)-(56.575, 72.5)` is shortened to `(52.965, 72.5)-(55.05, 72.5)`, the track `(56.575, 72.5)-(58.1, 70.975)` becomes `(55.05, 72.5)-(56.575, 70.975)`, and one track `(56.575, 70.975)-(58.1, 70.975)` is added; (c) the `+5V_WB` via moves from (58.1, 56.8) to (58.1, 56.65) and its three tracks follow (`(63.18, 58.2)-(61.63, 56.65)`, `(61.63, 56.65)-(58.1, 56.65)` on B.Cu and `(58.1, 56.65)-(58.1, 54.955)` on F.Cu); (d) the reference text positions of T911 (`at -6.06 -0.16`), RV911 (`at 2.5 5.3`), and R911, R912 and R922 (`at 0 -2.3 90`, `at 0 -2.3 90`, `at 0 2.3 90`). No pad, outline line, drill, other zone, other footprint's geometry or library file differs. Judged by a reviewer that had not seen the board, from the design files, the checklist (the catalogue's 71 items and BOARD-1 to BOARD-18 of `layout-plan.md`, 89 rows), the pre-order check's lines, my own renders and the committed `layout-plan.md`; rounds 1 to 6 were read as history, not as evidence, and every verdict below was judged on the board and the records as this commit holds them. Round 7 is meant to close the review: it is full and judges the design files as they stand.
- **Views**: this round did not run `task hw:views` or `task hw:placement` (the disk had 7 GB free and the brief asked for a closer look than their 30 px/mm gives); every verdict rests on views rendered here from a detached worktree of 9e6bb8ee outside the repository, and the placement, assembly, 3D and net views of the method are therefore not in this round. Commands (KiCad 10.0.7, resvg and rsvg-convert, `KICAD_CLI=/Applications/KiCad/KiCad.app/Contents/MacOS/kicad-cli`):

```
kicad-cli pcb export svg --layers F.Cu,F.SilkS,Edge.Cuts --mode-single --exclude-drawing-sheet --page-size-mode 2 -o fc.svg heater-wall-board.kicad_pcb
kicad-cli pcb export svg --layers B.Cu,Edge.Cuts --mode-single --exclude-drawing-sheet --page-size-mode 2 -o bc.svg heater-wall-board.kicad_pcb
kicad-cli pcb export svg --layers F.SilkS,Edge.Cuts --mode-single --exclude-drawing-sheet --page-size-mode 2 -o fs.svg heater-wall-board.kicad_pcb
rsvg-convert -w 2024 -b "#101010" fc.svg -o fc.png     (the same for bc and fs: 44.99 pixels per millimetre, board x 20 to 65, y 21 to 96)
python3 tiles.py                                       (cuts 28 tiles T<row><column>, each three panels side by side)
rsvg-convert -w 4498 fc.svg -o fc100.png               (100 pixels per millimetre, four crops)
```

  **Tiles looked at, every one, as images at 45 pixels per millimetre.** The grid is 4 columns by 7 rows, 11.25 mm by 10.71 mm, each cut 0.4 mm wider on every side; column c0 is x 19.6 to 31.65, c1 x 30.85 to 42.9, c2 x 42.1 to 54.15, c3 x 53.35 to 65.4; row r0 is y 20.6 to 32.1, r1 y 31.3 to 42.8, r2 y 42.0 to 53.5, r3 y 52.7 to 64.3, r4 y 63.5 to 75.0, r5 y 74.2 to 85.7, r6 y 84.9 to 96.4. Each tile shows three panels at the same coordinates: F.Cu with F.SilkS over it, B.Cu, and F.SilkS alone. The 28 tiles looked at: T00 T01 T02 T03, T10 T11 T12 T13, T20 T21 T22 T23, T30 T31 T32 T33, T40 T41 T42 T43, T50 T51 T52 T53, T60 T61 T62 T63. Also looked at as images at 100 pixels per millimetre (F.Cu with F.SilkS): `z_wedge` (x 47 to 61, y 33 to 48, the new OUT_C shape), `z_via` (x 55.5 to 65, y 53.5 to 63, the moved via, J931 and the U921 pads), `z_t911` (x 51 to 61, y 21.5 to 31.5) and `z_t921` (x 29 to 39, y 21.5 to 31.5), the two identical triac chains; and two overlays at 45 pixels per millimetre that colour each silk pixel by what lies under it (`ov_bottom`, x 20 to 65, y 74 to 96; `ov_mid`, x 20 to 65, y 40 to 62). Not rendered as images: F.Mask, F.Paste, B.Paste, F.Fab, F.CrtYd (read through the board file: the mask openings follow the pads, the six F.Fab keep-out rectangles were listed from the file, no paste layer carries a via). Computed from the board file and not seen as images: the interior angle at every vertex of the eight zone outlines (the four keep-out rule areas excluded) and of every filled polygon, the shortest edge of each outline, every track's length and direction, the 12 clearance findings of KiCad's own check (`kicad-cli pcb drc`, all severities, 0 unconnected items), and the silk census (80 graphics at 0.15 mm or more, 38 texts of 1.0 mm or more at strokes of 0.15 mm or more).
- **Pre-order check**: the whole-repository check, `python3 hardware/tools/board_check.py --root .` (the command `task hw:verify` runs), all seven designs in one run from the detached worktree under `nice`, KiCad 10.0.7, ended with the line RESULT, PASS, all 7 designs passed, exit 0, with 0 `VIOLATION` lines of any kind in the whole report. Counts by kind over the whole report: 0 `VIOLATION`; 7 `DESIGN` (all PASS); 319 `ACCEPTED` (14 `derating_exception`, 88 `spacing_exception`, 217 `workmanship_acceptance`); 265 `WARNING`, all main board (96 `designator_astray`, 70 `reference_off_silkscreen`, 64 `test_point_courtyard`, 17 `ground_return`, 13 `silk_edge_clearance`, 3 `silk_overlap`, 2 `track_width`); 107 `FIGURE`; 7 `COUNT`: each equal to round 6's. The wall board's `DESIGN` line and every `FIGURE` line under it (no `VIOLATION` line; 28 `ACCEPTED` lines, 24 `spacing_exception` and 4 `derating_exception`, no `WARNING`):

### Plan and free pass

**Plan against layout.** The floorplan holds: the six rule areas stand at the plan's bounds on both copper layers (read from the board file's zone polygons); the connector table holds (J941, J921 and J911 at x 24.50, 32.60 and 44.00, y 90.04; J931 pin 1 at (63.18, 58.20)); the outline is (20.0, 21.0) to (65.0, 96.0), 45.0 by 75.0 mm, with the two 1.5 mm slots open at the right edge; H1 at (61.50, 92.50); all 15 footprints on the front. The plan's `yes` rows and the board's own items were read against the tiles and the board file, each by its verdict row. The plan's own rows that name a spur at U921 pin 4, a via on the OUT_S spur at (57.85, 78.25) and a `HEAT_S_OPTO_MT` run to pin 6 are baseline rows written before placement, and the amendment notes and the later passes carry what the board became (the OUT_S track is the 2.1 mm run at y 77.2 and the one via is on +5V_WB); no row's departure is new in this round.

**What changed since round 6, read from the files.** Only the blocks the opening lines list. What each does, judged on the board as it stands:

- The front OUT_C zone. The outline's vertices are now `(56.45, 25.4)` and the 45-degree run to `(53.4, 28.45)`, straight down x 53.4 to `(53.4, 35.9)`, a 45-degree run of 4.1 mm to `(49.3, 40)`, straight down x 49.3 to `(49.3, 46.425)`, along y 46.425 to `(58.1, 46.425)`, up x 58.1 to `(58.1, 36.2)`, a 0.424 mm 45-degree run to `(58.4, 35.9)` and up x 58.4 to the neck at pad 2. My own computation of the interior angle at each vertex gives 90 degrees at the two bottom corners, 135 degrees at each 45-degree chamfer's outer corner and 225 degrees at its inner corner, and nothing under 90 degrees in the outline or in the filled polygon: the acute notch whose tip stood at `(49.875, 44.425)` is gone, and there is no concave notch or sliver left (the shortest non-arc edges of the outline are the 0.424 mm step and the 0.636 mm neck chamfer). The diagonal now ends at `(49.3, 40)`, the height of RV911 pad 1's centre `(50.5, 40)`, and the vertical edge x 49.3 is tangent to that pad's left edge (pad radius 1.2), as `z_wedge` shows, so the pad joins solid with no copper left behind it. The body below y 36.2 is 8.8 mm wide (x 49.3 to 58.1) and ends at y 46.425, the pad edge of U911 pin 6; U911's pad 6 projects 0.75 mm past the body's right edge x 58.1 at y 44.43 to 46.43, a 90-degree step that is the pad's own outline. All of it stays 1.5 mm or more from every other net, which the DRC bears out (its 12 findings are the accepted 1.04 mm pad pairs). One thing is raised: the 0.3 mm offset of the right edge, finding 7.2.
- The `HEAT_S_OPTO_MT` run. R921 pad 1 `(52.965, 72.5)` to U921 pin 4 `(58.1, 70.975)` now leaves the pad straight (2.085 mm), turns once at 45 degrees (2.157 mm) and enters the pad along its axis at its centre height (1.525 mm): three tracks, 5.767 mm, ratio 1.077 against the straight line, all runs over 1 mm, all at 0, -45 and 0 degrees, as `T42` and `T43` show. Nothing to raise.
- The +5V_WB via, at `(58.1, 56.65)`: 0.345 mm from the bottom of U921 pin 1's pad and 0.35 mm above the ring of J931 pin 3 (`z_via`), so it sits centred in that gap; its three tracks end on its centre; the DRC reports it and its tracks clean. Seen and not raised: the top line of J931's silk outline (y 56.74) passes through the via's lower half; the board tents both faces and the via has no mask opening, so nothing is printed over copper that is exposed (rounds 4 and 6 judged the same at y 56.8).
- The labels. T911 `(48.6, 22.7)`, RV911 `(48.0, 34.7)`, R911 `(59.7, 38.5)`, R912 `(59.7, 28.5)` and R922 `(40.9, 28.5)`, each computed from its property offset and its footprint's angle, equal `fabrication-notes.md`; the three resistor references read upward (90 degrees); none stands on copper or inside a courtyard or touches another text (`T01`, `T02`, `T03`, `T12`, `T13`).

**Dead copper, necks, asymmetry, tested.** Every zone has one filled polygon per layer and no island (read from the file); every track ends on a pad, a via, a zone or another track of its net (KiCad's `track_dangling` is clean); no zone has copper behind a pad that nothing joins. The necks are the two triac pin-2 waists of 0.58 mm (accepted, not raised), the 0.98 mm finger of OUT_S that carries only the opto-triac's gate current (OUT_S_F0 x 62.95 to 63.93, y 70.0 to 77.2), the B.Cu tab of RET at RV911 (3.5 mm) and the chamfers into the pads (0.52 mm and 0.64 mm); none is new. The two chains are identical in their parts and differ in copper only as the circuit requires: T911 and T921 stand at the same pad row; the gate tracks `HEAT_C_TRIAC_G` and `HEAT_S_TRIAC_G` leave pin 3 at 45 degrees to a 0.5 mm column at x 62.0 and x 38.6 (`z_t911`, `z_t921`), R912 and R922 are the same resistor turned the same way with their references on the side away from the strips; the references of T911 and T921 stand mirrored (left of T911, right of T921, 6.06 mm and 11.14 mm from their origins, y 22.7). OUT_C's front zone shifts to the left toward its blade and has a right edge at x 58.4 to 58.1 and a left chamfer of 4.31 mm, where OUT_S's front zone is a symmetrical 5.0 mm strip; that is the plan's routing (BOARD-4), not a leftover.

**Silkscreen, tile by tile.** Every F.SilkS text and graphic was looked at on all 28 tiles and the two overlays. Alignment: the J931 legend is right-aligned to x 55.55 on 1.5 mm rows, the board title block is right-aligned to x 64.3, the blade names are centred over their blades (x 24.5, 32.6, 44.0) with the function names 2.0 mm below, the pin-1 dots stand at the pin-1 corners, the hazard block is centred on x 44.45 on bare laminate. Labels over copper edges: five legends are printed across the edges of the copper under them; they are raised once as finding 7.1 (the legend `OUT_S` is 6.6 mm wide across a 5.0 mm strip and crosses both of its edges; `OUT_C` rests its underscore on the 0.2 mm top of the J911 pad copper; `COFFEE` crosses both copper edges by 0.2 mm and 0.1 mm; `RETURN` ends 0.1 mm or less inside the copper's right edge; the reference `RV921` crosses the RET strip's edge, the 2.7 mm gap and the OUT_S strip's edge; the Staircase logo's left edge is tangent to the right edge of the J911 copper at x 46.55). Seen and not raised: the footprint outlines of the blades, RV911, RV921 and the triacs stand across zone edges because their parts sit on the copper they join (`ov_bottom`, `ov_mid`).

**Every closure of an earlier round re-tested against the board as it stands.** The copper of the heavy strips is unchanged since round 3 and the changed copper only removed area or moved a signal track, so the reasons of 1.1, 1.2, 1.3, 1.4, 1.7 and 1.8 still apply and their fail rows cite them (POWER-2, POWER-3, POWER-5, CONN-4, WORK-1, BOARD-4 and BOARD-5; H1 measures 14.3 mm from the OUT_S end cap, over 13.45 mm, so 1.4's `overridden by: BOARD-11` holds). 1.5 and 1.6: the rows are in `design-rules.md`. 1.9: six dashed rectangles on F.Fab, listed from the file. 1.10, 2.1, 2.4, 3.1 to 3.3: the silk anchors equal the records. 2.3: relaxation R1 states the 47.5 mm run, 4.7 mm and 3.8 K, and the OUT_C back zone is unchanged. 4.1, 4.2, 5.1, 6.1, 6.2: the statements they closed are gone or amended and 4.2's silk strokes are all 0.15 mm or more (79 graphics at 0.15 mm and the hazard triangle at 0.25 mm, 80 in all).

**Stale statements.** Sweeps over `hardware/sunbeam-em7000` and `docs/sunbeam-em7000`, `*.md` and `*.yaml`, `layout-review.md` and `schematic-review.md` read as history, several phrasings each: the front OUT_C extents (`ends at the finger`, `44.425`, `49.875`, `wedge`: 0 hits; `y 44.4 to 46.4`, `44.4`, `46.4`: hits only in the frozen Pass 1 row MAINS-2 of `layout-plan.md` line 326 and in frozen rounds 2, 3 and 6 of this record, finding 7.4; `finger`: the `MainsBranch` row of `design-rules.md` (the OUT_S track "ends in a zone finger", true) and the plan's rows, all of the OUT_S finger); the via (`56.8`, `56.65`, `58.1, 56`: only frozen round 1 to 3 text; `design-rules.md` names the one via on +5V_WB and its sizes only); the `HEAT_S_OPTO_MT` route (named in net lists and the plan's baseline rows only); the label positions (`34.7`, `22.7`, `38.5`, `28.5`, `59.7`, `40.9`, `reading upward`: `fabrication-notes.md` is the one place and agrees with the board file); the copper record (the board holds 22 locked items, 14 tracks and 8 zones, on 11 critical-class nets, as `sweep-log.md` says; none of the other records states a count); silk (38 texts and 80 graphics agree with the board file, `Silkscreen line width` agrees); `45 x 75`, `45 x 76`, `76 mm` (only the frozen WORK-9 baseline row with its amendment note); `MOC3083SVM`, `MOC3083SR2M`, `DIP-6` (the records name the V option and the SMDIP-6 footprint; the stale through-hole phrases are gone since 4.1); `47.5 mm`, `4.7 mm`, `3.8 K` (agree with the board); `W-12` to `W-15` (the header's 18 entries agree with W-1 to W-15 with W-5 split in four; W-14 and W-15 agree with their commits). Two live statements are findings: finding 7.3 (the closing round named in `owner-sign-off.md`) and finding 7.4 (the pass-3 section of `sweep-log.md`).

**Free pass.** Seen on the tiles and not asked by any item: the legends over copper edges (finding 7.1); the 0.3 mm step (finding 7.2); the via under J931's silk line (above, not raised); the back carries only the order-number placeholder, over bare laminate; the hazard block on laminate with no F.Cu copper under it; the pin-1 dot of U921 is 0.25 mm from the board edge's centre line, as round 6 recorded, with no silk-edge warning on this board; the maker mark clear of every pad. Two statements and two shapes are findings: 7.1 to 7.4. Not judged and why: `hw:views` and `hw:placement` were not run (above); the maker's capability page was not fetched again (no Board Setup line changed since round 6, which fetched it on 2026-11-20); `hw:review-history` fails the four disclosed checks and `closing round` until this round is recorded; the `PinCoverageProof` test failure on origin/main is not this board's; silk text height has no measured rule yet (carried); the 0.95 mm TO MAIN BOARD row distance, the 0.20 mm triac annulus, the 0.58 mm waist under each triac pin 2, RV911's off-centre tab, the OUT_S 43.4 degree chamfer, the J931 table legend and mains copper 1.0 mm from the top edge are accepted and not raised again.

### Findings

| Finding | Raised by | Severity | What and where | Rule | Added as | Planning shortfall | Closed by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 7.1 | free pass | cosmetic | Silk legends and one reference are printed across the edges of the copper under them, so each reads half on laminate and half on mask-covered copper. Measured on `T50`, `T51`, `T52`, `T20`, `T62` and the overlay `ov_bottom` and `ov_mid` (silk pixels over the F.Cu raster) and from the board file anchors: `OUT_S` (1.5 mm text centred at (32.6, 80.3), about x 29.3 to 35.9) is 6.6 mm wide across the 5.0 mm OUT_S strip (x 30.0 to 35.0) and crosses both edges (0.7 mm past the left, 0.9 mm past the right); `OUT_C` (centred at x 44.0, y 79.5 to 81.1) puts its underscore on the top 0.2 mm of the J911 copper that starts at y 80.9; `COFFEE` (centred at (44.0, 82.3), x 41.34 to 46.65) crosses both edges of the J911 copper (x 41.55 and 46.55) by 0.2 mm and 0.1 mm; `RETURN` ends 0.1 mm or less inside the RET copper's right edge x 27.3; the reference `RV921` (x 26.3 to 30.8, y about 45.3) crosses the RET strip's edge x 27.3, the 2.7 mm gap and the OUT_S strip's edge x 30.0; the left edge of the Staircase logo at x 46.55 is tangent to the J911 copper's right edge. The board works and the maker prints silk over mask-covered copper, so this is appearance only: a letter half over the copper step shows the step through the mask. Fix: keep each legend wholly on bare laminate or wholly inside one copper feature, for example `OUT_S` at 1.0 mm text (about 4.4 mm wide) centred on x 32.5, `OUT_C`, `COFFEE` and `RETURN` moved clear of the copper's top and side edges by 0.3 mm or more, `RV921` put wholly on the RET strip or on bare laminate, the logo moved 0.3 mm right; every anchor and the fabrication-notes figures then change together. | no: a measurement could state it (each silk text's box against the front copper's outline, an inherent zero) but the maker's capability page states no silk-to-copper-edge figure for mask-covered copper, so the rule has no source outside the check (step 5's case); the figure would need a published silk-over-copper guide | MFG-12 (a new judgement-kind item of the manufacture area, cosmetic: a silk legend or reference stands wholly on bare laminate or wholly inside one copper feature; Source this review, round 7 finding 7.1; to be added to the method in the fixing change) | — | Change, `56660c80`, critical routing pass 4: the blade legends are turned to read upward in pairs, `RET` at (23.475, 79.9) and `RETURN` at (25.6, 79.9), `OUT_S` at (31.575, 79.9) and `STEAM` at (33.7, 79.9), `OUT_C` at (38.45, 82.0) and `COFFEE` at (40.575, 82.0); the `RV921` reference stands upward at (28.65, 35.7); the maker block (36 items) moved 0.5 mm in x. 12 silk crossings of copper edges remain, all of them part-body outlines drawn by library footprints (the three blade rectangles, the T911 and T921 bodies, the RV911 and RV921 bodies, the J931 box): by reason, not by change, they are normal component outlines, and cutting them needs library edits that raised 8 `lib_footprint_mismatch` violations; the statement is in `design-rules.md` and `fabrication-notes.md`. MFG-12 is not added to the method here. |
| 7.2 | free pass | cosmetic | The right edge of the front OUT_C zone `OUT_C_F0` steps 0.3 mm inward at `(58.4, 35.9)` to `(58.1, 36.2)` (a 0.424 mm 45-degree run), so the 5.0 mm strip above (x 53.4 to 58.4) and the 8.8 mm body below (x 49.3 to 58.1) have right edges 0.3 mm apart. Seen at `z_wedge` (100 px/mm) and `T13` at the right of the zone; it is the step the pass-3 commit left when it filled the old wedge, and it serves a purpose only below y 44: the lower body's edge is held at x 58.1 by the 1.5 mm class clearance to U911 pin 5's pad (left edge x 59.89, 1.79 mm from x 58.1 and 1.49 mm from x 58.4), while above y 36 no copper of another net stands within 3.3 mm of x 58.4 (R911's gate column is at x 62.0), so the strip's x 58.4 is not what sets the step. It is the same kind of offset as the jog `WORK-5` forbids in tracks (no run under 1 mm), here in a zone outline, and a reviewer working at high zoom sees a small kink in an otherwise straight edge. Fix: draw the right edge as one straight line at x 58.1 from the neck chamfer down to the pad 6 edge at y 46.425 (the strip above is then 4.7 mm wide and the neck chamfer shortens to 0.2 mm), or make the change of width one 45-degree taper of 1 mm or more; a straight edge at x 58.4 all the way down is not the fix, since it would stand 1.49 mm from U911 pin 5's pad, under the 1.5 mm class clearance. Re-run the DRC, refill and lock. | no: WORK-5's 1 mm is a track figure with no source for zone outlines, and applied to outlines it would flag the 0.42 mm to 0.64 mm chamfers at the triac necks and the OUT_S 43.4 degree chamfer, which are accepted shapes; the figure needs a published guide on copper-pour edge jogs | WORK-11 (a new judgement-kind item of the workmanship area, cosmetic: a zone outline carries no offset or step under 1 mm other than a 45-degree chamfer at a corner or a neck; Source this review, round 7 finding 7.2; to be added to the method in the fixing change) | — | Change, `56660c80`, critical routing pass 4: the right edge of `OUT_C_F0` is one straight line at x 57.95 from the neck to the pad 6 edge at y 46.425, the left edge of the strip moved from x 53.4 to x 52.95 with its chamfer starting at (56.45, 24.97), and the zone outline has 43 vertices where it had 51. WORK-11 is not added to the method here. |
